PANEL AB-28.9  (docs/diagrams/agentbox/28-agentbox-services-and-manifest-binary.md)
## AB-28.9 Service crate publishing posture

```mermaid
classDiagram
    class PublishableServiceCrate {
        <<ADR-2030>>
        +LICENSE-APACHE
        +LICENSE-MIT
        +README.md
        +Cargo.toml with repository metadata
        +inline rustdoc on every public item
    }
    class agentbox_manifest {
        boot-time TOML/JSON projection
        clap subcommands
        golden tests
    }
    class agentbox_ops {
        retired-Python tool suite
        15 binaries
        12 shared modules
    }
    class agentbox_mcp {
        3 stdio MCP servers plus the hub
        rmcp transport
    }
    class skill_tools {
        uiux · wardley · docs_alignment
        12 binaries
    }
    class podcast_ingest {
        corpus ingest
        see AB-27
    }
    class explainer_tools {
        explainer-loom-draft
        LICENSE-APACHE and LICENSE-MIT
        see AB-28.13
    }
    class AgplException {
        <<NOT dual-licensed>>
        +LICENSE with the full AGPL-3.0 text
        +README stating it is not dual-licensed
    }
    class dream_engine {
        nightly cycle, AGPL-3.0-only
        links nostr-bbs-core
        see AB-23
    }
    class headroom_napi {
        NAPI compression addon
        crates/headroom-napi, AGPL-3.0-only
        see AB-26
    }
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- agentbox_manifest
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- agentbox_ops
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- agentbox_mcp
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- skill_tools
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- podcast_ingest
    PublishableServiceCrate <|-- explainer_tools
    AgplException <|-- dream_engine
    AgplException <|-- headroom_napi
    note for PublishableServiceCrate "ADR-2030 permissive licensing for publishable service crates. Dual LICENSE-APACHE +<br/>LICENSE-MIT with a README are recorded for agentbox-manifest, agentbox-mcp, agentbox-ops,<br/>explainer-tools, podcast-ingest and skill-tools (LICENSING-NOTICE.md:42-51).<br/>ontology-tools left the set with its crate, deleted by ADR-2119 (2026-10-01)"
    note for AgplException "dream-engine is AGPL-3.0-only because it links nostr-bbs-core (LICENSING-NOTICE.md:14-20, :46);<br/>headroom-napi lives under crates/, outside services/, and its manifest also declares<br/>AGPL-3.0-only (crates/headroom-napi/Cargo.toml:8). Neither inherits the ADR-2030 posture"
    note for agentbox_manifest "Nix packaging for each crate lives in agentbox/lib — agentbox-manifest.nix,<br/>agentbox-ops.nix, agentbox-mcp.nix, skill-tools.nix, explainer-tools.nix,<br/>podcast-ingest.nix, dream-engine.nix, headroom-compress.nix. Only dream-engine is<br/>manifest-gated out of the package set (flake.nix:1761-1768); agentbox-manifest,<br/>agentbox-ops and the knowledge-tool crates are UNGATED and always baked<br/>(flake.nix:1749-1752, flake.nix:1770-1774, flake.nix:1793-1812)"
    note for agentbox_ops "Each binary replaces a Python script retired by the 2026-09-02 estate legacy audit;<br/>the modules hold the behaviour worth unit-testing independently of the CLI shell<br/>(agentbox-ops/src/lib.rs:1-5)"
    note for skill_tools "docs_check_mermaid is the skill-side mermaid validator, now shelling pele-render --check not mmdc (docs_alignment/mermaid.rs:1-6, ADR-2154);<br/>this diagrams tree is validated instead, and more strictly, by VisionFlow's<br/>scripts/diagram-index-gen.cjs --render, which renders every block in-process with the pinned Pele"
```

**Drift (corrected 2026-11-02):** this panel had `dream-engine` and `headroom-napi` inheriting the ADR-2030 permissive posture, but both declare `AGPL-3.0-only` (`../project/agentbox/services/LICENSING-NOTICE.md:46`, `../project/agentbox/crates/headroom-napi/Cargo.toml:8`); it counted 12 binaries and 9 modules for `agentbox-ops` where the pin has 15 and 12; and it said every crate is in the package set only when its gate is on, where only `dream-engine` is gated (`../project/agentbox/flake.nix:1761-1768`) and the rest are baked unconditionally (`../project/agentbox/flake.nix:1749-1750`).

     54  Ten package manifests, seven permissive and three AGPL (2026-09-25:
     55  `dream-engine` moved to AGPL-3.0-only; `explainer-tools` had been added without

     58  copyright holder and year range match the repository `NOTICE`:
     59  `Copyright (c) 2024-2026 DreamLab AI / Dr John O'Hare`. The Apache-2.0 text is
     60  the canonical upstream text with only the appendix boilerplate copyright line
     61  filled in; the MIT text is the canonical SPDX `MIT` text.

     63  Each manifest also declares `description`, `repository` and `readme`, which is
../project/agentbox/services/LICENSING-NOTICE.md @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 2-32:
      2  
      3  The crates in this directory are licensed under either of
      4  
      5  - MIT License (`LICENSE-MIT` in each crate directory), or
      6  - Apache License, Version 2.0 (`LICENSE-APACHE` in each crate directory),
      7  
      8  at your option, as declared in each crate's `Cargo.toml`. This differs from
      9  the rest of the repository, which is AGPL-3.0-only under the root `LICENSE`.
     10  The split is deliberate and recorded in `docs/adr/ADR-2030`: these crates are
     11  self-contained, clean-room modules meant for reuse and publication to
     12  crates.io, while the repository as a whole remains a copyleft hosted service.
     13  
     14  Three exceptions, for two different reasons. A crate that links an AGPL-licensed
     15  library cannot grant permissive terms and declares `AGPL-3.0-only` in its own
     16  manifest (`nostr-pod-bridge`, which links `solid-pod-rs-nostr`; `dream-engine`,
     17  which links `nostr-bbs-core` for Nostr signing and the forum governance wire
     18  types — operator decision 2026-09-25, ADR-2115; since ADR-2141 it is only the
     19  adapter, and the gate and loop crates it consumes are a separate permissive
     20  repository, `DreamLab-AI/dream-engine`). A crate that is
     21  deliberately not offered for reuse declares `AGPL-3.0-only` with
     22  `publish = false` (`secret-backup`, which handles operator secret material and is
     23  scoped to this container rather than to crates.io — its dependency graph is
     24  wholly permissive, so this is a choice, not a constraint). All three ship the full
     25  AGPL text as `LICENSE` and a README that states in terms that they are **not**
     26  dual-licensed, so neither can be published under the permissive assumption.
     27  
     28  Contributions to this subtree are accepted under the same MIT OR Apache-2.0
     29  terms unless a crate's manifest says otherwise; contributions to
     30  `nostr-pod-bridge`, `dream-engine` and `secret-backup` are accepted under
     31  AGPL-3.0-only.
     32

   1753  
   1754          # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   1755          # dream-engine — nightly evidence-gated repo evolution (ADR-052 the connected node annexe).

**Invariant (proposed):** the identity key never spends and never seals, which is what keeps a compromised wallet from being a compromised identity (`../project/agentbox/docs/INGRESS-identity.md:506`).

../project/agentbox/docs/INGRESS-identity.md @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 494-518:
    494  surface, so this governing document moves in the same change as the records. The Invariants

    496  
    497  ### Three keys, domain separated (ADR-2101 D3)
    498  
    499  sidestr upstream reuses one raw private key as Nostr identity, taproot spending key and block
    500  sealing key, with no domain separation, so one compromise yields all three. The estate refuses
    501  that shape and already owns the tool to avoid it: HMAC-SHA256 domain-separated child derivation
    502  with a JS-parity vector (`nostr-bbs-core keys.rs:251-265`).
    503  
    504  | Key | Derivation | Held by | May |
    505  |---|---|---|---|
    506  | `k_id` | the sovereign identity key (`services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/identity.rs:130-158`) | the identity binary | sign identity events, sign the 38420 binding. **Never spends, never seals a block.**
    507  | `k_spend(chain)` | `derive_subkey(k_id, "sidestr/spend/" ‖ chain_id)` | the wallet path | spend UTXOs on exactly that chain |
    508  | `k_sign(chain)` | `derive_subkey(k_id, "sidestr/sign/" ‖ chain_id)` | federated instance operators only | seal blocks on exactly that chain |
    509  
    510  Per-chain derivation is what makes a leaked child-chain spend key unable to touch root coins and a
    511  compromised session unable to seal root blocks. The peg descriptor never contains an identity key,
    512  and the nsec never enters the settlement domain: signing happens behind the identity port.
    513

    517  never inferred:

../project/agentbox/management-api/lib/sidestr-spend-key.js @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 1-24:
      1  'use strict';
      2  
      3  /**
      4   * sidestr-spend-key — the per-agent spend key `k_spend` and its binding to the
      5   * identity key `k_id` (ADR-2101 D3/D4, ADR-2097 D3 as amended 2026-10-02).
      6   *
      7   * `k_id` is the agent's did:nostr key (agent-identity.js). It never spends.
      8   * `k_spend` is a second secp256k1 key, minted at spawn beside `k_id` from an
      9   * independent random seed (nostr-tools `generateSecretKey`), never derived
     10   * from `k_id`: a leaked spend key reveals nothing about the identity, and an
     11   * identity key reachable by every supervised program cannot reach the coins.
     12   * It is stored the way `k_id` is stored: 64 hex in a 0600 file in the identity
     13   * directory, one per chain:
     14   *
     15   *   <identityDir>/agent-spend-<chain name>-<did hex>.key
     16   *
     17   * The binding is the kind-38420 `sidestr-account-binding` event
     18   * (PROTOCOL-registry, ADR-2098 D2): addressable, signed by `k_id`, content the
     19   * spend pubkey, `d` = `<chain id>:<did hex>`, where the chain id is the 64-hex
     20   * id of the chain's kind-3500 event, or its genesis hash for a chain sealed
     21   * before sidestr 0.0.5 (then tagged `legacy`). It is written beside the key as
     22   * `<key>.binding.json` and published to relays fail-open; a payee inlines it in
     23   * its 402 offer so a payer can verify who it pays without a relay round trip.
     24   *

     95  **Everything below `38400` is spoken for.** The phrase "the free `38106`-`38201`
     96  range", which carried ADR-2085's allocation and the settlement pack's, was wrong:
     97  `38100`-`38199` is the agent-response reservation in its entirety, and a consumer
     98  that reads the range (the relay consumer does) cannot tell a reserved agent
     99  response from a knowledge unit. `38200`-`38299` and `38300`-`38399` are band
    100  reservations in the host registry, not two and six loose numbers. `38400`-`38499`
    101  is the first hundred no record reserves, so new agentbox kinds come from there.

   1801  # ── Workspace trust seed: pre-accept Claude Code's folder-trust dialog ──
   1802  # The "Do you trust the files in this folder?" gate blocks any unattended
   1803  # session (tmux teammate panes, background agents, cron `claude -p`) started
   1804  # in a checkout that has not been trusted yet (2026-09-02: ten worker panes

../project/agentbox/services/agentbox-manifest/src/mcp.rs @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 131-155:
    131  /// Mirror one server from `from` (the workspace `.mcp.json`) into `to` (the
    132  /// user-level `~/.claude/.claude.json`), so it loads whatever directory Claude
    133  /// Code is started in.
    134  ///
    135  /// `.mcp.json` is only discovered by walking up from the cwd, so a session
    136  /// started outside `$WORKSPACE` (for example in `$HOME`) never saw the
    137  /// `claude-flow` RuVector memory server. The user-level file is read from every
    138  /// cwd. Only `mcpServers[name]` is touched; every other key Claude Code keeps
    139  /// in that file survives, and the file is rewritten only when the entry
    140  /// actually differs (the log line follows suit). A missing source entry is a
    141  /// no-op, an unparseable destination is left alone rather than clobbered, and
    142  /// the destination is made owner-only because the entry carries the conninfo.
    143  pub fn mirror_server(from: &Path, to: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    144      let src = load_strict(from)?;
    145      let Some(spec) = src.get("mcpServers").and_then(|s| s.get(name)) else {
    146          return Ok(());
    147      };
    148      let mut dst = if to.exists() {
    149          jsonio::read_opt(to)
    150              .ok_or_else(|| format!("{}: unparseable, left untouched", to.display()))?
    151      } else {
    152          Value::Object(Map::new())
    153      };
    154      let servers = jsonio::mcp_servers_mut(&mut dst);
    155      if servers.get(name) == Some(spec) {

   3832  # carried the password, and the first-write guards above never rewrite them. This

   3843    node <<'PGSCRUBJS' || true
   3844  // PGSCRUB_MARKER
   3845  const fs = require('fs');
   3846  const path = require('path');
   3847  const e = process.env;
   3848  const pwFile = e.PGS_FILE || '/run/secrets/ruvector-pg/password';
   3849  const parts = {
   3850    RUVECTOR_PG_HOST: e.RUVECTOR_PG_HOST || 'ruvector-postgres',
   3851    RUVECTOR_PG_PORT: e.RUVECTOR_PG_PORT || '5432',
   3852    RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE: e.RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE || 'ruvector',
   3853    RUVECTOR_PG_USER: e.RUVECTOR_PG_USER || 'ruvector',
   3854    RUVECTOR_PG_PASSWORD_FILE: pwFile,
   3855  };
   3856  const MAX_BYTES = 32 * 1024 * 1024;
   3857  let scrubbed = 0;
   3858  let failed = 0;
   3859  
   3860  // Atomic rewrite: a sibling temp file (0600, same directory), fsync, rename over the
   3861  // original. A kill before the rename leaves the original untouched; the temp file only
   3862  // ever holds the already-scrubbed text, so it never carries the password.
   3863  function atomicWrite(f, data, mode, uid, gid) {
   3864    const tmp = path.join(path.dirname(f), `.${path.basename(f)}.pgscrub.${process.pid}`);
   3865    let fd = null;
   3866    try {
   3867      fs.rmSync(tmp, { force: true });
   3868      fd = fs.openSync(tmp, 'wx', 0o600);
   3869      fs.writeSync(fd, data);
   3870      fs.fsyncSync(fd);
   3871      // The rename replaces the inode, so carry the original owner across (this runs as root at boot).
   3872      if (uid !== undefined) { try { fs.fchownSync(fd, uid, gid); } catch { /* not permitted: keep the writer's owner */ } }
   3873      fs.fchmodSync(fd, mode);
   3874      fs.closeSync(fd); fd = null;
   3875      if (e.PGS_TEST_FAIL_BEFORE_RENAME === '1') throw new Error('injected failure before rename');
   3876      fs.renameSync(tmp, f);
   3877      try { const dfd = fs.openSync(path.dirname(f), 'r'); try { fs.fsyncSync(dfd); } finally { fs.closeSync(dfd); } } catch { /* best effort */ }
   3878    } catch (err) {
   3879      if (fd !== null) { try { fs.closeSync(fd); } catch { /* ignore */ } }
   3880      try { fs.rmSync(tmp, { force: true }); } catch { /* ignore */ }
   3881      throw err;
   3882    }
   3883  }
   3884  // Read a regular (non-symlink) file of sane size; null if it is anything else.
   3885  function readRegular(f) {
   3886    try {
   3887      const st = fs.lstatSync(f);
   3888      if (!st.isFile() || st.size > MAX_BYTES) return null;
   3889      const text = fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8');
   3890      return text.includes('\u0000') ? null : { text, mode: st.mode & 0o777, uid: st.uid, gid: st.gid };
   3891    } catch { return null; }
   3892  }
   3893  // Text-level removal of the RuVector password from one string (hook command, URL, conninfo, raw
   3894  // file text). Scoped to RuVector's own parts so another database's credentials in the same file
   3895  // (an operator's unrelated MCP server) are left alone.
   3896  const esc = (v) => String(v).replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
   3897  const CONN_ASSIGN = /RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO=(?:"[^"]*"|'[^']*'|[^\s"';&|]+)/g;
   3898  const URL_PW = /(postgres(?:ql)?:\/\/)([^\s:\/@"']*):([^\s@"']+)@([^\s\/:?"']+)(:\d+)?(\/[^\s?"']*)?/gi;
   3899  const KV_PW = /[ \t]*\bpassword=(?:'[^']*'|"[^"]*"|[^\s"']+)/gi;
   3900  const RV_KV = new RegExp(`\\b(?:host=${esc(parts.RUVECTOR_PG_HOST)}|user=${esc(parts.RUVECTOR_PG_USER)}|dbname=${esc(parts.RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE)})(?![\\w.-])`);
   3901  function cleanText(s) {
   3902    let o = s.replace(CONN_ASSIGN, `RUVECTOR_PG_PASSWORD_FILE=${pwFile}`);
   3903    o = o.replace(URL_PW, (m, pre, user, _pw, host, port, db) => {
   3904      const dbn = String(db || '').replace(/^\//, '');
   3905      const ours = host === parts.RUVECTOR_PG_HOST || user === parts.RUVECTOR_PG_USER || dbn === parts.RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE;
   3906      return ours ? `${pre}${user}@${host}${port || ''}${db || ''}` : m;
   3907    });
   3908    if (RV_KV.test(o)) o = o.replace(KV_PW, '');
   3909    return o;
   3910  }
   3911  // 1. A parsed JSON document (.mcp.json, Claude Code's per-user state and its backups, settings
   3912  //    snapshots): every mcpServers entry whose env holds a conninfo, memory.password, and any string
   3913  //    value (hook commands) carrying a conninfo or credentialled URL. Returns the entries changed.
   3914  function scrubDoc(node, top, isCf) {
   3915    let n = 0;
   3916    if (!node || typeof node !== 'object') return 0;
   3917    if (top && isCf && node.memory && typeof node.memory === 'object' && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(node.memory, 'password')) {
   3918      delete node.memory.password; n++;
   3919    }
   3920    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(node)) {
   3921      if (k === 'mcpServers' && v && typeof v === 'object') {
   3922        for (const srv of Object.values(v)) {
   3923          if (srv && srv.env && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(srv.env, 'RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO')) {
   3924            delete srv.env.RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO;
   3925            Object.assign(srv.env, parts);
   3926            n++;
   3927          }
   3928        }
   3929      }
   3930      if (typeof v === 'string') {
   3931        const c = cleanText(v);
   3932        if (c !== v) { node[k] = c; n++; }
   3933      } else {
   3934        n += scrubDoc(v, false, false);
   3935      }
   3936    }
   3937    return n;
   3938  }
   3939  function scrubFile(f, forceMode) {
   3940    const r = readRegular(f);
   3941    if (r === null) return;
   3942    const mode = forceMode === undefined ? r.mode : forceMode;
   3943    let out = null;
   3944    let n = 0;
   3945    try {
   3946      const j = JSON.parse(r.text);
   3947      n = scrubDoc(j, true, f === e.PGS_CF || path.basename(path.dirname(f)) === '.claude-flow');
   3948      if (n) out = JSON.stringify(j, null, 2);
   3949    } catch {
   3950      // TOML (Codex) or unparsable text: the conninfo line becomes the parts + the file path
   3951      let t = r.text;
   3952      if (/^RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO\s*=/m.test(t)) {
   3953        const lines = Object.entries(parts).map(([k, v]) => `${k} = ${JSON.stringify(v)}`).join('\n');
   3954        t = t.replace(/^RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO\s*=.*$/m, lines);
   3955      }
   3956      t = cleanText(t);
   3957      if (t !== r.text) { out = t; n = 1; }
   3958    }
   3959    if (out === null) return;
   3960    try { atomicWrite(f, out, mode, r.uid, r.gid); scrubbed += n; } catch { failed++; }
   3961  }
   3962  // Live targets
   3963  const live = [e.PGS_MCP_JSON, ...String(e.PGS_CLAUDE_JSON || '').split(':'), e.PGS_CODEX, e.PGS_CF].filter(Boolean);
   3964  for (const f of live) scrubFile(f, f === e.PGS_CODEX || f === e.PGS_CF ? undefined : 0o600);
   3965  // 2. Snapshots of the projected config: Claude Code's rolling .claude.json backups, and the
   3966  //    operator/reconcile copies (*.pre-*, *.bak*). Content scrubbed, files kept.
   3967  const SNAP_SUFFIX = /\.(?:pre-[^/]+|bak[^/]*|backup[^/]*)$/;
   3968  const KNOWN_BASE = /^(?:settings(?:\.local)?\.json|\.claude\.json|\.mcp\.json|config\.(?:json|toml)|claude_desktop_config\.json)\./;
   3969  const anyDirs = new Set(String(e.PGS_ANY_DIRS || '').split(':').filter(Boolean));
   3970  const dirs = new Set([...String(e.PGS_SNAP_DIRS || '').split(':').filter(Boolean), ...live.map((f) => path.dirname(f))]);
   3971  const liveSet = new Set(live);
   3972  for (const d of dirs) {
   3973    let names = [];
   3974    try { names = fs.readdirSync(d); } catch { continue; }
   3975    for (const nm of names.sort()) {
   3976      if (nm.includes('.pgscrub.') || !SNAP_SUFFIX.test(nm)) continue;
   3977      if (!(anyDirs.has(d) || KNOWN_BASE.test(nm))) continue;
   3978      const f = path.join(d, nm);
   3979      if (!liveSet.has(f)) scrubFile(f);
   3980    }
   3981  }
   3982  console.log(scrubbed ? `  [security] removed a stored Postgres password/conninfo from ${scrubbed} config entr${scrubbed === 1 ? 'y' : 'ies'}` : '  [security] no stored Postgres password/conninfo found
   3983  if (failed) console.log(`  [security] WARNING: ${failed} file(s) could not be rewritten; rotate the RuVector password`);

PANEL AB-36.3  (docs/diagrams/agentbox/36-role-isolation-and-identity-port.md)
## AB-36.3 /run/secrets: its own root mount, one sealed directory per role

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    MNT["/run/secrets tmpfs mode=711 uid=0 gid=0 noexec nosuid nodev<br/>docker-compose.yml:111, flake.nix:3723<br/>its OWN mount: devuser owns /run but cannot rename a mount point"]
    MNT --> MODE{"[security].role_isolation"}
    MODE -->|"off"| OFF["chown 1000:1000, chmod 0700 as before (entrypoint-unified.sh:359)<br/>devuser's /run/secrets/nostr.key is written as today"]
    MODE -->|"on"| PREP["ab_secrets_root_prepare: root 0711 (role-custody.sh:89)<br/>not a mount point: ROLE-ISOLATION-DEGRADED secrets-mount (role-custody.sh:99)"]
    PREP --> CAP["_ab_role_env_capture: each ROLE variable to /run/secrets/role/NAME 0400,<br/>then unset from PID 1 (entrypoint-unified.sh:460, list at entrypoint-unified.sh:382)"]
    CAP --> DEL["ab_role_secrets_deliver runs the plan (entrypoint-unified.sh:1284, role-custody.sh:218)"]

        P1["root (role_accounts.rs:1034)"]
        P2["role: dir 0500 plus home 0700 (role_accounts.rs:1036)"]
        P3["env: write PID 1's VAR, 0400, unset (role_accounts.rs:1039)"]
        P4["file: copy an at-rest source, 0400 (role_accounts.rs:1041)"]
        P5["sockdir ab-identity-port 0750, devuser-owned ab-sessions skipped (role_accounts.rs:1048-1050)"]
        P6["dir: receipts dir outside the mount (role_accounts.rs:1057)"]
        P1 --> P2 --> P3 --> P4 --> P5 --> P6
    end
    DEL --> PLAN
    PLAN --> SEAL["seal each role dir last: 0500 owned by the role (role-custody.sh:351-353)"]
    SEAL --> SCRUB["_ab_role_env_scrub: any ROLE variable still present is a LEAK, unset (entrypoint-unified.sh:1295)"]
    SCRUB --> X["exec supervisord (entrypoint-unified.sh:1296)"]
    MNT --> PG["BOTH modes, outside the role plan: _ab_pg_credential_prepare makes a devuser-owned<br/>ruvector-pg/ 0700 with the RuVector password 0600 inside this mount<br/>(entrypoint-unified.sh:473-475, entrypoint-unified.sh:486-488, entrypoint-unified.sh:507)<br/>OPEN: the rehearsal was not re-run with it present<br/>(ADR-2122-role-service-accounts-run-secrets-and-the-identity-port.md:772)"]
```

**What it shows.** Under the flag, Stage A turns every role secret into a 0400 file in a 0500 directory owned by its role, inside a mount only root can write. The variables it came from leave PID 1's environment before supervisord starts, so no child inherits them. With the flag off, the same mount is handed to devuser as before. **Why it is this way.** `/run` is a tmpfs owned by uid 1000, so devuser can rename anything directly under it, root's entries included. Only a separate mount point is safe (ADR-2122's correction, `ADR-2122-role-service-accounts-run-secrets-and-the-identity-port.md:357-364`). Delivery runs before supervisord, so no devuser process exists yet to race it, and it fails open with a counted, grep-able marker, leaving a role program with no secret to fail closed under supervisord (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:11-14`).

**Invariant:** under role_isolation, the runtime copy of each role secret is readable only by its role. The directory is sealed `0500` to the role (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:353`) inside a root `0711` mount (`docker-compose.yml:111`).

**Invariant (role_isolation vs the at-rest volume):** the flag confines the at-rest copies as well as the runtime ones. Under it the boot skips the devuser chown of the `agentbox-secrets` volume root (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:567`) and calls `ab_custody_migrate` on the baked plan (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:742`). The plan's `atrest` rows are every resolved file source plus each role's `at_rest` entries, and its `atrestdir` rows the table's `at_rest_dirs` (`role_accounts.rs:1066-1083`, `config/role-accounts.json:146-149`). `ab_custody_migrate` (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:447`) copies a missing canonical copy once from its legacy twin, hands every copy to its role at 0400 (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:556`) and each volume dir to root (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:567`); with the flag off `ab_custody_revert` (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:586`) hands them back (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:744`). The faucets' sources are now the volume copies, the workspace files their legacy twins (`config/role-accounts.json:78`). Those twins are never deleted and stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge (`ADR-2122-role-service-accounts-run-secrets-and-the-identity-port.md:241-244`).

    462  # RuVector Postgres credential custody. The password enters the container once,
    463  # as RUVECTOR_PG_PASSWORD (compose requires it; there is no default). It is
    464  # written to ONE devuser-owned 0600 file, and the variable is unset before
    465  # anything below can copy it into a config, a hook command or an exported env.
    466  # Everything downstream gets the public parts (host/port/database/user) and the
    467  # FILE PATH, nothing else; every database client assembles its connection in
    468  # management-api/lib/pg-config.js. Independent of role isolation: with the flag
    469  # on /run/secrets is root 0711, so this keeps its own devuser-owned subdirectory.
    470  # A password that is neither in the environment nor already on disk fails closed:
    471  # the error names the file path, no default is invented, and the memory clients
    472  # then refuse to start (they read the same file).
    473  _ab_pg_credential_prepare() { # _ab_pg_credential_prepare <secrets-root> <uid:gid of the reader>
    474    local root="${1:-/run/secrets}" owner="${2:-1000:1000}" dir file tmp
    475    dir="${root}/ruvector-pg"; file="${dir}/password"
    476    # Exported first, so the projections below never expand an unset name under
    477    # `set -u` even when the password itself is unavailable (clients then fail
    478    # closed on the named path).
    479    export RUVECTOR_PG_PASSWORD_FILE="$file"
    480    export RUVECTOR_PG_HOST="${RUVECTOR_PG_HOST:-ruvector-postgres}"
    481    export RUVECTOR_PG_PORT="${RUVECTOR_PG_PORT:-5432}"
    482    export RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE="${RUVECTOR_PG_DATABASE:-ruvector}"
    483    export RUVECTOR_PG_USER="${RUVECTOR_PG_USER:-ruvector}"
    484    if [ -n "${RUVECTOR_PG_PASSWORD:-}" ]; then
    485      if [ -L "$dir" ] || { [ -e "$dir" ] && [ ! -d "$dir" ]; }; then rm -f -- "$dir"; fi
    486      mkdir -p -- "$root"; [ -d "$dir" ] || mkdir -m 0700 -- "$dir"
    487      chown "$owner" "$dir" 2>/dev/null || true

../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2122-role-service-accounts-run-secrets-and-the-identity-port.md @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 345-376:
    345  - **The socket group comes from the table.** `config/role-accounts.json` gains `groups`
    346    (`ab-identity-port`, gid 969, members devuser, `ab-identity` and `ab-gateway`; the owner is a
    347    member because chgrp needs membership) and `dirs`. `isolate` exports
    348    `AGENTBOX_IDENTITY_SOCK_GID` to `ab-identity`'s programs, and the plan gains `sockdir` and `dir`
    349    rows. Role groups still have no members.
    350  - **Deviation: the socket is not under `/run/agentbox`.** The disposition asked for a root-owned
    351    `/run/agentbox`. `/run` is a devuser-owned tmpfs, so devuser could rename a root-owned
    352    `/run/agentbox` away and plant a socket. That is the reason `/run/secrets` has its own mount
    353    (§"Deviations"). Root-owning it would also break `bootstrap-seal` (`bootstrap.done`, the
    354    `/ready` signal) and `teammate-gc` under the flag. The socket therefore sits inside the
    355    `/run/secrets` mount.
    356  
    357  ## Correction to the integration disposition: the socket's directory — 2026-10-03
    358  
    359  The queen's integration disposition (design §13) placed the identity socket in a root-owned
    360  `/run/agentbox`. That does not hold, because `/run` is devuser's. It is a tmpfs mounted
    361  `uid=1000,gid=1000` (`docker-compose.yml:109`, `flake.nix:3533`). A non-root owner of a
    362  directory can rename any entry in it, so devuser can move a root-owned `/run/agentbox` aside and
    363  plant a directory with its own socket. Ownership of the entry does not matter; ownership of the
    364  parent does. `/run/secrets` is safe for the same reason in reverse: it is its own tmpfs,
    365  `uid=0,gid=0,mode=711` (`docker-compose.yml:110`, `flake.nix:3538`), so it is a mount point
    366  devuser can neither rename nor write. The socket's directory, `/run/secrets/ab-identity-port`,
    367  sits inside it. Root-owning `/run/agentbox` would also have broken two devuser writers under the
    368  flag: `bootstrap-seal`, whose `/run/agentbox/bootstrap.done` is the `/ready` signal
    369  (`config/seal-bootstrap.sh:15`), and `teammate-gc`.
    370  
    371  The queen accepted the correction. The rehearsal now tests the property. In check (a), devuser
    372  tries to rename the socket directory and to create an entry beside it, and both must be refused.
    373  A directory that is renamed is moved straight back. `tests/config/role-isolation-rehearsal.test.sh`
    374  case 17 puts the socket directory under a devuser-writable parent, the `/run/agentbox` case,
    375  and shows (a) failing with the directory restored. The PASS fixture shows both attempts refused.
    376  
../project/agentbox/config/lib/role-custody.sh @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 1-26:
      1  # shellcheck shell=bash
      2  # config/lib/role-custody.sh — /run/secrets delivery for per-role service
      3  # accounts (ADR-2122, custody X-1 step 1, W1). Sourced by
      4  # config/entrypoint-unified.sh; definitions only, nothing runs on source.
      5  #
      6  # Every function here is reached only when [security].role_isolation = true,
      7  # except the two pickers, which return today's values for any other input. So
      8  # with the flag off the boot is what it was before this file existed.
      9  #
     10  # Contract (the entrypoint relies on all of it):
     11  #   * fail-open: no function returns non-zero to a `set -e` caller for a
     12  #     delivery problem; failures are counted in AB_RC_FAILURES and logged with a
     13  #     grep-able [security] marker. A missing secret makes its role program fail
     14  #     closed under supervisord, which is where it belongs, not the boot.
     15  #   * no secret VALUE is ever printed, logged, or passed on argv. File sources go
     16  #     through `cat <path>`; environment values through the `printf` builtin
     17  #     inside a subshell, which forks but never execs, so no argv carries them.
     18  #   * runs before supervisord, so no devuser process exists yet to race a
     19  #     check-then-copy. Symlinked, non-regular or oversized sources are refused
     20  #     anyway, because the workspace bind persists across boots.

    575      # mkdir there.

```mermaid
    Note over HP,NS: the POST writes NostrService, the GET reads ProtectedSettingsActor — the two stores never meet
    Note over PS: handlers with NO sender anywhere in src or crates at this revision: ValidateClientToken (src/actors/protected_settings_actor.rs:49),<br/>StoreClientToken (:65), UpdateUserApiKeys (:81), CleanupExpiredTokens (:97), MergeSettings (:112),<br/>SaveSettings (:127), GetUser (:142)
    Note over PS: DEBT — a second copy of the actor lives at crates/visionclaw-actors/src/protected_settings_actor.rs:7,<br/>AppState starts the src/actors copy. Session-token realm and get_session expiry see VC-03.5 and VC-05
```

**Tension:** `POST /api/auth/nostr/api-keys` stores keys on the `NostrService` user record (`../project/src/handlers/nostr_handler.rs:293`, `../project/src/services/nostr_service.rs:495`). `GET` on the same resource reads `ProtectedSettingsActor` (`../project/src/handlers/nostr_handler.rs:319`, `../project/src/app_state.rs:1578-1586`), which starts from `ProtectedSettings::default()` (`../project/src/app_state.rs:1159`), whose `users` map is empty (`../project/crates/visionclaw-domain/src/models/protected_settings.rs:132`), and has no writer. On source evidence a non-power user never reads back the keys they saved. This needs a runtime check to confirm.

**Drift:** the panel this replaces drew the api-keys routes driving all eight actor messages. At this revision only `GetApiKeys` has a sender, and seven handlers are unreachable.

    484      pub async fn update_user_api_keys(
    485          &self,
    486          pubkey: &str,

    235  fn now_wire_ts() -> u32 {
    236      let ms = std::time::SystemTime::now()
    237          .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
    238          .map(|d| d.as_millis() as u64)
    239          .unwrap_or(0);
    240      wire_clock(ms)
    241  }

    247      type Result = ();
    248  
    249      fn handle(&mut self, msg: UpdateBotsGraph, _ctx: &mut Self::Context) -> Self::Result {
    250          let events = match self.feed.lock() {
    251              Ok(mut f) => {
    252                  let events = f.apply(msg.source, &msg.agents, &msg.targets, now_wire_ts());
    253                  info!(
    254                      "AgentBeamActor: roster from {:?}: {} live agents, {} frame(s)",

    256                      f.len(),
    257                      events.len()
    258                  );
    259                  events
    260              }
    261              Err(_) => return,
    262          };
    263          self.broadcast(events);
    264      }

    106  
    107  /// `POST /api/bots/update` and `POST /api/bots/data`: the authenticated REST roster
    108  /// producer (the dev beam driver). The agents are embodied through `0x23` frames by the
    109  /// real-agent producer (ADR-2142 amendment 2026-11-01); the reply is the roster view.
    110  pub async fn update_bots_graph(
    111      _auth: crate::settings::auth_extractor::AuthenticatedUser,
    112      request: web::Json<BotsDataRequest>,
    113      state: web::Data<AppState>,
    114  ) -> Result<impl Responder> {
    115      info!("Received bots roster update with {} agents", request.nodes.len());
    116  
    117      state.graph_service_addr.do_send(UpdateBotsGraph {
    118          source: RosterSource::Rest,
    119          agents: request.into_inner().nodes,
    120          targets: Vec::new(),
    121      });
    122      let view = match state.graph_service_addr.send(GetBotsGraphData).await {
    123          Ok(Ok(view)) => view,
    124          _ => Arc::new(GraphData::new()),
    125      };
    126  
    127      ok_json!(BotsResponse {
    128          success: true,
    129          message: "Roster accepted".to_string(),
    130          nodes: Some(view.nodes.clone()),
    131          edges: Some(view.edges.clone()),
    132      })
    133  }
    134  
    135  /// `GET /api/bots/data`: the live roster, one record per agent keyed by its `0x23` wire id,

    137  /// panels and docks; agents are drawn from `0x23` frames, never from this (ADR-2142).

PANEL VC-36.4  (docs/diagrams/visionclaw/36-godot-xr-client.md)
## VC-36.4 Per-socket independent reconnect backoff

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Disconnected
    Disconnected --> Connecting: open() or retry timer fires
    Connecting --> Backoff: connect error or failed topology-request send, both surface as Disconnected
    Connecting --> Subscribed: requestInitialData sent, only then connection_changed true
    Subscribed --> Backoff: socket closed or recv error, surfaced as Disconnected
    Backoff --> Connecting: timer expires
    Backoff --> Backoff: presence only, kicked from presence calls the scheduler directly
    Subscribed --> Closed: close() - no reconnect
    note right of Subscribed
        The client has no Authenticated state.
        NIP-98 authenticate is sent after Connected
        and its acceptance is never read back, transport.rs:113-120
    end note
    note right of Backoff
        GRAPH, per BackendConnection: backoff_delay backend_connection.gd:83-84
        min(RECONNECT_BASE_DELAY_SEC * 2^(max(attempts,1)-1), RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY_SEC)
        base 2.0 s backend_connection.gd:26, cap 60.0 s backend_connection.gd:27
        scheduled on a drop only while open backend_connection.gd:355-356, :340-343
        timer counted down in poll backend_connection.gd:137-142
        attempts reset on connected true backend_connection.gd:347-349
        PRESENCE, on the scene: _backoff_delay graph_scene.gd:4169-4173
        same 2.0 s / 60.0 s constants graph_scene.gd:36, :37
        presence timer graph_scene.gd:4178, kicked entry graph_scene.gd:4553
        INVARIANT every socket backs off INDEPENDENTLY: one
        BackendConnection per source plus the presence socket
        graph_scene.gd:890-892, backend_connection.gd:8-10
    end note
```

**Debt:** the presence socket keeps its own copy of the backoff (`xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:4169-4180`) beside `BackendConnection.backoff_delay` (`xr-client/scripts/backend_connection.gd:83-84`). The two formulas differ only at `attempts` 0, which the presence path never passes.
**Drift:** the comment in `_on_client_connection_changed` says a drag-end pin persists across a reconnect (`xr-client/scripts/backend_connection.gd:353-354`), but a drag end records no pin since ADR-2172 F3 (`xr-client/scripts/backend_connection.gd:273-275`).

   4159  func _tick_reconnect(delta: float) -> void:
   4160  	# Each socket owns its own countdown; firing one reconnects only that socket
   4161  	# so a pending graph reconnect can't tear down a live presence socket.
   4162  	if _presence_reconnect_timer >= 0.0:
   4163  		_presence_reconnect_timer -= delta
   4164  		if _presence_reconnect_timer <= 0.0:
   4165  			_presence_reconnect_timer = -1.0
   4166  			_connect_presence()
   4167  
   4168  
   4169  func _backoff_delay(attempts: int) -> float:
   4170  	return minf(
   4171  		RECONNECT_BASE_DELAY_SEC * pow(2.0, float(attempts - 1)),
   4172  		RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY_SEC
   4173  	)
   4174  
   4175  
   4176  func _schedule_presence_reconnect() -> void:
   4177  	_presence_reconnect_attempts += 1
   4178  	_presence_reconnect_timer = _backoff_delay(_presence_reconnect_attempts)
   4179  	push_warning("GraphScene: presence reconnect attempt %d in %.1fs" % [
   4180  		_presence_reconnect_attempts, _presence_reconnect_timer])

     24  
     25  # The dot(camera_forward, dir_to_agent) above which the user is deemed to be
     26  # gazing at an agent — a ~25° head-gaze cone (cos 25° ≈ 0.906), feeding the
     27  # agent's mutual-gaze model.
     28  const MUTUAL_GAZE_DOT: float = 0.9
     29  
     30  # HTTP origin for the intervention decide POST, derived from the ws:// backend
     31  # base by scheme swap (ws→http, wss→https) unless XR_BACKEND_HTTP overrides.
     32  
     33  # Reconnect with exponential backoff, unbounded: a headset that goes to sleep
     34  # (SteamVR standby on the Vive today; a Quest in its case once the standalone
     35  # build ships) must rejoin when it wakes, however long that takes.
     36  const RECONNECT_BASE_DELAY_SEC: float = 2.0
     37  const RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY_SEC: float = 60.0
     38  
     39  # Render budgets, sized for the lowest-spec headset we intend to support
     40  # (Quest 3 standalone, a planned target) and therefore comfortably safe on the
     41  # currently deployed Vive/SteamVR desktop rig.
     42  # When the graph exceeds these, the most important nodes (by server-computed
     43  # centrality) and the heaviest edges (by weight) are kept — same importance
     44  # language as the desktop client.
     45  # Instance budgets are now RUNTIME-DERIVED from the received topology (the backend
     46  # serves a settings-driven initial load that can exceed any fixed constant), not
     47  # hardcoded quality gates. Node budget = topology node count, edge budget =
     48  # topology edge count, each bounded by an absolute safety ceiling so a runaway

   4166  			_connect_presence()

   4170  	return minf(
   4171  		RECONNECT_BASE_DELAY_SEC * pow(2.0, float(attempts - 1)),
   4172  		RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY_SEC
   4173  	)

   4159  func _tick_reconnect(delta: float) -> void:
   4160  	# Each socket owns its own countdown; firing one reconnects only that socket
   4161  	# so a pending graph reconnect can't tear down a live presence socket.
   4162  	if _presence_reconnect_timer >= 0.0:
   4163  		_presence_reconnect_timer -= delta
   4164  		if _presence_reconnect_timer <= 0.0:
   4165  			_presence_reconnect_timer = -1.0
   4166  			_connect_presence()
   4167  
   4168  
   4169  func _backoff_delay(attempts: int) -> float:
   4170  	return minf(
   4171  		RECONNECT_BASE_DELAY_SEC * pow(2.0, float(attempts - 1)),
   4172  		RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY_SEC
   4173  	)
   4174  
   4175  
   4176  func _schedule_presence_reconnect() -> void:
   4177  	_presence_reconnect_attempts += 1
   4178  	_presence_reconnect_timer = _backoff_delay(_presence_reconnect_attempts)
   4179  	push_warning("GraphScene: presence reconnect attempt %d in %.1fs" % [
   4180  		_presence_reconnect_attempts, _presence_reconnect_timer])

../project/src/services/role_store.rs @ 86cdd7635582 lines 1-17:
      1  //! Persisted pubkey → role store for multi-user RBAC (ADR-142).
      2  //!
      3  //! Backs [`crate::models::rbac::UserRole`] with a dedicated `user_roles` SQLite
      4  //! table. The table lives in the same database file as settings (it shares the
      5  //! already-open [`tokio_rusqlite::Connection`]) but is a **separate, isolated
      6  //! table** — deliberately *not* the user-writable `settings` owner layer — so a
      7  //! user cannot escalate their own role through any settings-write endpoint.
      8  //!
      9  //! Identity is the NIP-98-verified pubkey (the user's DID), canonicalised to
     10  //! lowercase hex by [`canonicalise_pubkey`] on **every** path (get/set/remove/
     11  //! bootstrap/lookup) so a mixed-case input always resolves the same row. A
     12  //! process-global handle ([`global_role_store`]) lets `verify_access` resolve
     13  //! roles without threading a new parameter through every call site; it is
     14  //! installed once at startup from `main.rs`.
     15  
     16  use std::sync::Arc;
     17

PANEL AB-17.1  (docs/diagrams/agentbox/17-agent-events-and-provenance-bridge.md)
## AB-17.1 The agent-event wire envelope — single canonical builder

```mermaid
classDiagram
    class AgentEventPublisher {
        <<agentbox/management-api/utils/agent-event-publisher.js:26 extends EventEmitter>>
        +Set subscribers
        +List~object~ eventBuffer
        +int maxBufferSize
        +int nextEventId
        +subscribe(callback) unsubscribe
        +emitAgentAction(event)
        +createMcpNotification(event)
    }
    class AgentActionType {
        <<plain object, not frozen, agent-event-publisher.js:17>>
        QUERY 0 blue
        UPDATE 1 yellow
        CREATE 2 green
        DELETE 3 red
        LINK 4 purple
        TRANSFORM 5 cyan
    }
    class WireEvent {
        <<params.event in createMcpNotification:305>>
        +int version
        +int id
        +string source_agent_id
        +string target_node_id
        +int action_type
        +string action_type_name
        +int timestamp

        +string failure_mode

    }
    class Notification {
        <<JSON-RPC 2.0 frame>>
        +string jsonrpc
        +string method
        +object params
        +int message_type
        +int protocol_version
        +string timestamp
    }
    AgentEventPublisher --> AgentActionType : numeric action_type, string name derived by reverse lookup
    AgentEventPublisher --> WireEvent : builds
    WireEvent --* Notification : params.event
    note for AgentEventPublisher "INVARIANT — createMcpNotification is the SINGLE canonical wire-envelope builder (agent-event-publisher.js:296-303).<br/>The /v1/agent-events/stream WebSocket and the deprecated MCP-TCP bridge emit through here,<br/>so ADR-013 identity attribution (source_urn / target_urn / pubkey) is kept on those paths.<br/>DRIFT - the live outbound forwarder does NOT: it sends the bare event (agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:147),<br/>which VisionClaw ingest rejects as Malformed. see ES-02, finding W2-01<br/>agentbox is the canonical schema source. VisionClaw mirrors this shape in src/agent_events/schema.rs, PARTIALLY:<br/>that struct has no failure_mode or authority_class field. see ES-02"
    note for Notification "method notifications/agent_action. message_type 0x23 AGENT_ACTION for binary-frame parity (agent-event-publisher.js:338).<br/>protocol_version 2. version 3 inside the event. eventBuffer is a ring capped at maxBufferSize 1000 — the oldest event is shift()ed out,<br/>but every event is first appended to the durable agentEventArchive, fail-open (agent-event-publisher.js:154-160). see AB-14"
    note for WireEvent "BYTE-COMPATIBILITY DISCIPLINE — every additive field (failure_mode REC-5, token_count/handoff_id/verification REC-3 CTC,<br/>authority_class REC-6 AC4) is emitted as null when absent, so an existing success-only consumer sees an unchanged frame.<br/>DIVERGENCE — source_urn/target_urn/pubkey are OPTIONAL in Phase 1 and only become required under fail-closed attribution in Phase 5. Phase 5 is not here."
```

**Drift:** the publisher comment says VisionClaw mirrors this envelope in `src/agent_events/schema.rs` (`agentbox/management-api/utils/agent-event-publisher.js:296-298`), but at VisionClaw `86cdd7635582` `AgentActionEnvelope` carries `source_urn`, `target_urn`, `pubkey`, `token_count`, `handoff_id` and `verification` and no `failure_mode` or `authority_class` field, so serde drops those two wire fields on ingest. The mirror covers the identity and CTC fields only (`agentbox/management-api/utils/agent-event-publisher.js:326`, `agentbox/management-api/utils/agent-event-publisher.js:335`).

     15

../project/agentbox/management-api/utils/agent-event-publisher.js @ c57d9c0508f4 lines 142-172:
    142      // container's own env fallback on the OTHER field — misattributing the
    143      // event to neither the real actor nor the container consistently. The
    144      // caller's identity wins whenever it supplied any part of it; the env
    145      // value is the last resort, applied to both fields together.
    146      if (fullEvent.source_urn == null && fullEvent.pubkey == null) {
    147        fullEvent.source_urn = process.env.AGENTBOX_URN
    148          || process.env.AGENTBOX_DID
    149          || null;
    150        fullEvent.pubkey = process.env.AGENTBOX_DID || null;
    151      }
    152  
    153      // Buffer the event
    154      this.eventBuffer.push(fullEvent);
    155      // Durably archive BEFORE eviction can occur, so the retained record and the
    156      // archived one never diverge. Fail-open: archiving never breaks an emit.
    157      try { agentEventArchive.append(fullEvent); } catch { /* archive is best-effort */ }
    158      if (this.eventBuffer.length > this.maxBufferSize) {
    159        this.eventBuffer.shift();
    160      }
    161  
    162      // Notify all subscribers
    163      this.subscribers.forEach(callback => {
    164        try {
    165          callback(fullEvent);
    166        } catch (err) {
    167          this.logger.error('Agent event subscriber error:', err);
    168        }
    169      });
    170  
    171      // Emit on EventEmitter for other listeners
    172      this.emit('agent_action', fullEvent);
